Where the two orders differ, the check counts `update with RETURNING whose rows came in the order of the key` or `update with RETURNING whose rows came in the reverse of the order of the key` under "reached: ", and the same two for a delete. The kind names the statement, so that an engine whose rows of one kind of statement never vary cannot pass on the rows of the other. The first part of `TestEngine_returning` requires all four, on the core and in memory.

   `TestConn_returning` pins the rest. The columns are the names as the table stores them and the declared types, also of a statement that changes no row. The count of changes is that of the statement without RETURNING, through `Exec` and `Query`. A statement logs one event, before its rows are read and none after, with the kind, the access, `statement.rows`, `statement.rows_read`, `commit.parent`, and `statement.direction` for an update and a delete. An update of three rows, and then a delete of them, each give both orders over 40 seeds, each order at least 5 times. A commit that the store refuses as too large (`simstore.Options.MaxSize`) gives an error that matches `ErrTooLarge` and no rows, and changes nothing, for an insert, an update, and a delete. A context that ends before the statement changes nothing, and one that ends after the commit does not end the rows, outside a transaction and in one. `Exec` gives the errors of `Query` for a column of RETURNING that the table does not have and for a failed constraint, the same count, and reads no row again for a delete. Open rows of a RETURNING refuse a statement in a transaction, and refuse none outside one. goqite's send and receive run as SQLite runs them, as far as the slices take them. `TestCheck_returning` has `Check` of a statement with RETURNING, `TestPair_returning` holds one to the rules of its values and of its arguments through `pair.step`, and `TestNewPlan_returning` pins the plans.

   `TestAccessOf_golden` holds two statements with RETURNING in each of goqite's three schemas: "receive by id", goqite's update of the message that it receives, and "delete a queue with RETURNING". `PathAccessOf` leaves the RETURNING of an update or a delete out, so that `reachChange` sees the access also where a column of RETURNING is missing. `stmttest.Sequence` writes RETURNING since the generated checks of slice 14, which are described below.

   The driver has `TestReturning` in `conn_test.go` of the root package, with RETURNING through `database/sql`, also a commit of unknown outcome, which must give no rows. The driver check in `internal/sqlite/crosscheck/drivercheck` runs two sequences of statements with RETURNING against mattn/go-sqlite3, through each call and in transactions.

   `TestEngine_subquery` in `subquery_test.go` has the scalar subqueries of slice 15, against SQLite. Its tables have a column of each type, an index that serves a subquery, a table of one column for `*`, and an empty table. Its selects, updates, and deletes run through `pair.step`, on the core and in memory, with no row, one row, rows that tie, a subquery of the table of the statement, the errors of the names in SQLite's order, a correlated name, `*` of more than one column, and LIMIT 0 and each kind of argument of LIMIT. They also run in a write transaction that commits and in one that rolls back, after a restart with an empty cache, and in a read-only transaction. One subtest runs every pair of the five types of a column and of the column of its subquery, one ends a subquery with its context, and one runs goqite's receive without its arithmetic on goqite's table. `TestNewPlan` pins the plans, and `TestEngine_report` the event.

   `pair.step` takes a statement with subqueries. Before the call, `pair.subqueriesOf` asks SQLite for the candidates of each subquery: the rows of its WHERE that tie with its first row under its ORDER BY, or every row without an ORDER BY, and NULL without a row. `Engine.ObserveSubqueries` of `export_test.go` gives the test the values that the engine computed, and each must be a candidate. SQLite then runs the statement with each subquery replaced by a `?` bound to the value of the engine (`subqueryCall.flatten`), and every later comparison reads that statement, so a value that a legal order puts first is compared as SQLite's own. Where a subquery has more than one candidate, the second instance of the pair uses the values of the first. The pair counts a value that was NULL, that of the one first row, and, of rows that tie, SQLite's first and another, under "reached: ", and `TestEngine_subquery` requires the last two. `subqueryRule` is the rule of types of such a statement. One subtest pins `row value misused` of `*` of more than one column in a select at SQLite's offset, also with the subquery on the left. The driver has `TestSubquery` in `conn_test.go` of the root package, with goqite's receive directly, prepared, and in a transaction.

   The generated tests and the simulation write scalar subqueries since the generated checks of slice 15, of 2026-11-24. One select, update, or delete in eight has subqueries in the mixed profile, and every one in the profile `subquery` (`Profile.Subquery`), two of them one time in six. A subquery reads the table of its statement or another. It finds no row, one row, or several, with an ORDER BY that decides its first row, with one that leaves rows that tie, or with none, and its LIMIT is 0, 1, or more. Some of its shapes are those that s4ql refuses: a correlated name, a column of another type, `*` of more than one column, and an argument of LIMIT that is no integer from 0. One update in two of the profile is goqite's receive (`Profile.Receive`), and one call in five runs the text of the last statement with a subquery again, as a prepared statement does, with each argument changed one time in two (`Profile.Again`). The README of `stmttest` has the odds. A sequence writes the subqueries through its hook `Generator.Inner`, and leaves `Generator.Subquery` at 0. `TestDifferential` runs 80 sequences of the profile, `TestConn_differential` 20, and `TestGenerated` of the driver check 60, and the default environment of `TestSimulation` has a cell of 8 seeds of it, and the hammer environment one of 40. Five checks are of slice 15:
   - **The counts.** `pair.reach` counts the kinds of a statement with subqueries on the statement flattened with the values that the instance computed, and NULL for those that it did not compute (`subqueryCall.flatten`). So the `reach*` functions read a statement without a subquery. `pair.reachSubquery` in `subquery_test.go` counts the kinds of the subqueries: the statement by its call and its outcome, by the kind of its transaction, with RETURNING, with two subqueries, as a text that ran before, and by each refusal of s4ql; each comparison by its operator and its side, and each subquery by its table, `*`, its LIMIT, the place of its parameters, and an index that serves it; the rows that a subquery whose value the instance computed finds, with an ORDER BY that decides the first, with NULL first, and among rows that tie, where it gave SQLite's first or another; an update or a delete whose subquery reads what it changes; and goqite's receive. A kind names the kind of its statement where the kinds differ by statement, also both outcomes of the draw among rows that tie. `TestDifferential.kinds` has 133 kinds of slice 15, `TestConn_differential.kinds` 8, `TestSimulation.kinds` 47, and `TestGenerated.kinds` of the driver check 40.
   - **The rule of a long literal.** It reads the literals of a subquery too, as the engine measures them where they are, before the types. Under the lowered length limit, an update or a delete with a literal of a subquery that is too long must give `string or blob too big`, and is excused as `excusedLongChange`. A select with one is compared with SQLite, and is excused as `excusedLongCondition` where SQLite gives no error. A correlated subquery is refused before the lengths, so the rule asks `correlatedRule` first, and the refusal of the name is checked and excused as `excusedType`. `TestPair_subqueryTooLong` pins the three.
   - **The simulation.** `Engine.ObserveSubqueries` gives its function the context of the statement, so the simulation records the values that the engine computed for each attempt, by the ID of the attempt (`attempt.subqueries`). `run.flattened` requires each recorded value to be among SQLite's candidates on the state before the statement, for a statement of its own, for a commit of the log, and for a statement of a transaction. SQLite then runs the statement with the values in place of the subqueries, so the states stay equal where the ORDER BY of a subquery leaves rows that tie. A value that is not a candidate is the violation that the statement "gives the subquery" a value, and SQLite one of others. s4ql's refusals of a correlated subquery and of a second subquery of `*` of more than one column are checked against SQLite on the state that the statement saw, as a refusal for a type is (`subqueryRefused`). `tallyOf` counts the statements with subqueries by kind and outcome (`subqueryKinds`), and `run.flattened` counts the values that it checks.
   - **The driver check.** Through `database/sql` the test cannot see which of the rows that tie s4ql took, so `TestGenerated` calls `Sequence.Decide` before its first call. Every subquery then has an ORDER BY that ends with the key of its table, its first row is decided, and a difference from mattn's SQLite is a failure. The statements that s4ql refuses and mattn's SQLite runs are checked against s4ql's rule (`pair.subqueryRefusal`), each with an excuse of its own: `excusedCorrelated` for a correlated subquery; `excusedSubqueryType` for a subquery of a column of another declared type than the column that it is compared with, which SQLite compares by affinity; `excusedSubqueryLimit` for an argument of LIMIT that is no integer from 0, which SQLite reads as `LIMIT (X <> 0)`; and `excusedWideSubqueries` for an update or a delete with two subqueries of `*` of more than one column, where SQLite gives its error for the one that its plan codes first and s4ql refuses the second. `excusedQuotedSubquery` is a name in double quotes that is no column, which mattn's SQLite reads as a string and the pinned build refuses: s4ql must fail on its names, and the statement does not run in SQLite.
   - **The planted bugs.** Nine bugs of `bugs.go` are of slice 15. `lastRow` gives the value of the last row of several, `firstNotNull` that of the first row whose value is not NULL, `ownChanges` makes an update or a delete compute its subqueries again after each row that it changes, `keepValue` keeps the value of the last run of a statement whose plan prints alike, `noRowMatches` makes a subquery that finds no row match every row whose column is not NULL, `outsideTx` makes a subquery of a write in a transaction read the newest state that is committed, `swapSubqueries` swaps the values of two subqueries, `limitZeroRow` gives a subquery with LIMIT 0 the value of its first row, and `orderIgnored` takes the first row of the access of the conditions alone, and not of the ORDER BY. `TestDifferential_plantedBugs` plants each but `outsideTx`, which only a write in a transaction that spans statements plants, and `TestDifferential` runs none. It plants them in the first 12, 30, 12, 12, 12, 12, 30, and 12 sequences of the profile `subquery`, where 10, 12, 8, 6, 12, 8, 12, and 9 find them, and requires 3, 4, 2, 2, 4, 2, 4, and 3. The own failure of six is a value of a subquery that is not among SQLite's candidates; `ownChanges` and `noRowMatches` are found by any failure, the rows, the count of changes, or the tables that differ from SQLite's. `TestSimulation_plantedBugs` plants all nine in the hammer environment with the profile `subquery`, where 184, 104, 138, 106, 192, 106, 150, 138, and 174 of 200 seeds find them, and 184, 104, 77, 106, 186, 60, 147, 138, and 174 by their own violation. That of `ownChanges` is a count of changes that differs in SQLite's run of the log or of the transaction, that of `noRowMatches` the rows of a select that differ from SQLite's, and that of the others a value that is not a candidate. The other seeds that find `outsideTx` find that the committed state lacks a table that the transaction created, which the storage core refuses, and those of `swapSubqueries` that a value of another type than its column, which the swap gave a condition after the check of the types, reaches the storage, which refuses it, or the engine's own comparison, which fails as an internal error.

   `TestEngine_arithmetic` and `TestNewPlan_arithmetic` in `expression_test.go` have the arithmetic of the piece of work arithmetic, and `TestPair_arithmetic` runs hand-written and generated statements with arithmetic against SQLite through the differential pair, by rules of their own. The driver has `TestArithmetic` in `conn_test.go` of the root package. The sequences of `stmttest` write arithmetic since the generated checks of arithmetic of 2026-11-25, so the generated tests, the simulation, and the driver check meet it. [The tests of arithmetic](#the-tests-of-arithmetic) has what each checks, and the planted bugs of arithmetic.

   `TestEngine_text` in `expression_test.go` has the text in expressions of the piece of work text functions, against SQLite, and the driver has `TestText` in `conn_test.go` of the root package. The sequences of `stmttest` do not write text yet, so the generated tests and the simulation do not meet it. [The tests of text](#the-tests-of-text) has what each checks.

   The generated tests and the simulation write RETURNING since the generated checks of slice 14, of 2026-11-23. One insert, update, or delete in five has the clause in the mixed profile, and one in two in the profile `returning` (`Profile.Returning`). The clause is `*` one time in three, and otherwise one to four columns of the table, of which some repeat and one in twelve is a name that the table may not have. A statement with the clause runs through `Query` seven times in eight, as a select does. The profile also aims at goqite: one update in two changes the row of a key, by a WHERE of the key alone (`Profile.ByKey`), as goqite's receive by id does, and one insert in three with a list leaves out a key that has a default (`Profile.KeyLeftOut`), as goqite's send does. Since the review of these checks, it also aims at three faults that its statements reached too rarely: one update in four moves the row of a key to a key that no insert gives (`Profile.NewKey`), one in eight changes every row of its table (`Profile.EveryRow`), and one table in two has a default on its first column and none on its last (`Profile.DefaultFirst`), where `*` shows the order of the columns. The profile weighs updates more than before, 12 against 7. The README of `stmttest` has the odds. A sequence writes the clause through its hook `Generator.Return`, and leaves `Generator.Returning` at 0. `TestDifferential` runs 80 sequences of the profile, `TestConn_differential` 80, and `TestGenerated` of the driver check 50, and the default environment of `TestSimulation` has a cell of 6 seeds of it, and the hammer environment one of 16. Four checks are of slice 14:
   - **The counts and the choice.** `reachReturning` counts each statement with RETURNING by its call, `Query` or `Exec`, and its outcome. It counts the clause by its form: `*`, names, a name twice, and a name that the table does not have. For a query that succeeds, it counts no row, one row, and several rows, each type of a column that the statement gives, NULL, and a column that the list of an insert leaves out, with a default and without. It also counts an update that sets the key or a column of an index, an update that fails on a constraint where its WHERE finds two rows or more, and goqite's send and its receive by id. It counts an update that gives the key of a row that it moved to another key, which no row had before ("update with RETURNING of the key that moves a row to another key"), and `*` of a table with a column without a default after one with a default, for an insert, an update, and a delete, each where the statement gives a row. `checkChoice` checks the access of an update and a delete with RETURNING as without it, through `engine.AccessOf`, which leaves the clause out. `TestDifferential.kinds` has 65 kinds of slice 14, and `TestConn_differential.kinds` has 16.
   - **The order of the rows.** `pair.checkReturned`, described above, counts the rows of an update and those of a delete with RETURNING that came in the order of the key and in its reverse, which is what the spec's rule "Unspecified row order varies" says varies. `TestDifferential` reached them 13 and 9 times for an update and 15 and 21 times for a delete, after the review of these checks on 2026-11-23, and `TestConn_differential` 6 and 6, and 6 and 7 times. Both tests require all four. An engine that draws the direction but always gives the rows of a delete in the forward order, or only those of an update, tried by hand, fails both tests on the least number of the reverse of that statement, and the two table tests of variation fail too. No comparison of rows sees it, so it is not among the planted bugs.
   - **The excuse of a row of RETURNING under the length limit.** SQLite writes each row of RETURNING into a record of its own before it gives the rows, and that record can be longer than the row's record in the table: where RETURNING names a column twice, and where a REAL column holds an integral value, which the table's record holds as an integer, 0 and 1 in no byte, and that of RETURNING in eight. Under the lowered length limit, SQLite refuses such a statement with `string or blob too big` where that record is longer than the limit, although the row fits, and changes nothing. s4ql gives the rows and makes the change, a known difference that [the todo](../../docs/todo/slice-14.md) records. `pair.returningTooBig` excuses it as `excusedReturningTooBig` in one shape only: the statement has RETURNING, the instance has the lowered limit, SQLite gives `string or blob too big` and s4ql no error, SQLite runs the statement without its RETURNING under the limit, in a savepoint that is rolled back, and SQLite's answer without the limit is s4ql's. The default run of `TestDifferential` met it once in the profile `returning` until the review of these checks aimed the profile, and meets it no more; the nightly's run of ten times its sequences met it in sequence 16832, on a REAL column. `TestPair_returning` pins the excuse: a delete, an update, and an insert whose RETURNING names a blob of 85 hundredths of the limit twice, and two inserts with `returning *` of an integral REAL beside a text of 995 bytes, must be excused once each, and leave SQLite's table and the engine's alike.
   - **The simulation and the planted bugs.** Outside a transaction, a statement with RETURNING whose `Query` returned without an error is committed, so it must be in the log, as every committed write. If its caller read its rows to their end, they must be the rows that SQLite gives when it runs the commit of the log on the state before it (`run.verify`). No row comes with an error, by the construction of `Conn.queryText`, which gives rows only where the statement and its commit returned nil; the simulation does not read rows after an error. In a transaction, the rows are compared with SQLite's on the state that the transaction saw (`run.verifyTxs`), and a transaction that is rolled back after its rows were read must leave nothing in the log. A write through `Query` whose rows were not read to their end is compared by its error alone. The simulation checks this rule on the engine. Through the driver, an outcome that is unknown and a commit that is applied while the statement's context ends or the database closes are checked by the table test `TestReturning` of the root package alone, and a commit that the store refuses by `TestConn_returning` on the engine alone. `TestSimulation.kinds` has 9 kinds of slice 14, which the simulation counts as said above. Five bugs of `bugs.go` are of slice 14. `rowsOfFailed` gives the rows of a statement of its own whose commit failed, without the error, `oldValues` gives the rows of an update as they were, `deleteNone` gives no row of a delete, `dropLast` leaves out the last row of several, and `starKeyFirst` gives `*` with the key first. `TestDifferential_plantedBugs` plants the last four in the first 30 sequences of the profile `returning`, where 21, 16, 13, and 16 find them, each by rows that differ from SQLite's, and requires 7, 5, 4, and 5. It cannot find `rowsOfFailed`, because no commit fails there. `TestSimulation_plantedBugs` plants `rowsOfFailed`, `oldValues`, and `dropLast` in the hammer environment with the profile `returning`, where 62, 149, and 143 of 200 seeds find them, 62, 54, and 125 by their own violation. That of `rowsOfFailed` is a committed statement that is not in the log. That of `oldValues` is rows of RETURNING that the caller read and that differ from those of the commit in the log, and that of `dropLast` rows of a transaction that differ from SQLite's.

   The generated tests and the simulation write ORDER BY and LIMIT since the generated checks of slice 13, of 2026-11-21. One select in four has an ORDER BY, and one in four a LIMIT, in the mixed profile. In the profile `order` it is three in four and one in two (`Profile.Order`, `Profile.Limit`). The profile aims its ORDER BY at the keys of the indexes of a table: the leading columns of a key, mixed directions, and the columns of a key with a gap, and one select in three of a table with an index has a WHERE and an ORDER BY drawn together (`Profile.Aimed`): goqite's receive, or a pin at the start or in the middle of a key and an ORDER BY of the rest. Half of the values of its inserts are one of two of their type, so that rows tie (`Profile.Ties`). The README of `stmttest` has the odds. A sequence writes them through its hook `Generator.Order`, and leaves `Generator.OrderBy` at 0. `TestDifferential` runs 80 sequences of the profile and `TestConn_differential` 20, the default and the hammer environment of `TestSimulation` each have a cell of 6 seeds of it, and `FuzzDifferential` and `FuzzDifferential_core` each have one of its seeds. Six checks are of slice 13:
   - **The counts and the choice.** `reachOrder` counts the ORDER BY by its number of columns, its directions, the type of each column, NULL, a column that the WHERE pins, and no WHERE. It counts the LIMIT by what it keeps of the rows, and each kind of argument of LIMIT that s4ql refuses, which the sequence draws for one call in two of a `limit ?`. For every select with ORDER BY that SQLite and s4ql take, also one without WHERE, `checkChoice` requires the access of the model `stmttest.ChosenAccess`, which ranks the candidates with an ORDER BY and a LIMIT as [the rules](#the-rules) do. The kind of a choice then ends in `, delivered forward`, `, delivered backward`, or `, sorted`. `reachKeyShapes` counts an ORDER BY that the access delivers around a pinned column in the middle of the key, and one of the key of an index with a gap, over rows where the gap shows, so that a key that took it for its own order would give the rows in another order. Goqite's receive with a pin, an index that delivers the order beside one that narrows a range, is required too.
   - **The access of the run.** `pair.checkAccess` compares, for every select that SQLite and s4ql take, the access that the run reports, `Rows.Access`, with the model's choice for the indexes as SQLite lists them: the same candidate, the same pins and bounds, and delivered, in the same direction, or sorted. `checkChoice` checks the rules on the indexes that SQLite lists, through `AccessOf`; this check covers the indexes that the run read itself. An engine that forces a path is not checked.
   - **The order of the rows.** `pair.checkOrder` checks every query that the engine and SQLite answer with two rows or more. The rows must be in the order of the access, as `Rows.Access` gives it with the direction that the engine drew: SQLite gives the same select with an ORDER BY of the key of the access, or, where the access sorts, of the ORDER BY of the select and then the primary key (`ordered.inOrderOf`). The check then counts what the spec's rule "Unspecified row order varies" says varies, each only where the rows of the other direction differ: a select without ORDER BY whose rows came in the order of the key of its access, and one in the reverse; a sorted select whose rows that tie came in the order of the primary key, and one in the reverse; and a select whose order the access delivers and whose rows that tie came in the order of the key of the access. `TestDifferential` requires all five kinds, and `TestConn_differential` the first two.
   - **The directions from the seed of the sequence.** `newInstanceDirected` gives the instance of a sequence of `TestDifferential` and `TestConn_differential` a seed of the directions that it derives from the seed of the sequence, unless `-directions` gives one. So the five kinds above are a function of the seeds, as every other count is, and a recount gives the same rows each time. The second instance of a pair still draws its seed.
   - **The excuse of a sort under the length limit.** Under the lowered length limit, SQLite refuses some selects that it sorts with `string or blob too big`, and s4ql gives the rows, which is the known difference of [the todo](../../docs/todo/slice-13.md). `pair.sortTooBig` excuses it as `excusedSortTooBig` in one shape only: the query of a select with ORDER BY, SQLite's answer `string or blob too big`, and rows of the engine that are SQLite's for the same select without the limit, in order. `TestPair_sortTooBig` pins the excuse and what it does not excuse. No sequence of the default run meets it, so its count is pinned at 0. The queries that the tests write themselves, such as the ranked query of `ordered.compare`, are sorts of SQLite's own, so they run without the length limit (`oracle.unlimited`). So do the selects by which `subqueryCandidates` gives the values that a scalar subquery with ORDER BY may have, of slice 15, unless a literal or an argument of the subquery is longer than the limit, which s4ql refuses: the nightly's run of `TestDifferential` met a subquery that SQLite refuses alone for its sort in sequence 25196, and `TestSubqueryCandidates` pins both sides. `excusedLongCondition` takes one case more since slice 13: a select with a blob literal in a condition that is longer than the limit and an argument of LIMIT that s4ql refuses. SQLite turns the literal into NULL and then gives `datatype mismatch` for the argument, where s4ql gives `string or blob too big` for the literal first.
   - **The simulation and the planted bugs.** The simulation compares a query with ORDER BY or LIMIT by `ordered.compare` too (`differs` in `simulation_test.go`), since a LIMIT that cuts may leave other rows than SQLite's. It takes the refusal of an argument of LIMIT by `typeRule`, and counts five kinds of ordered and limited selects. Five bugs of `bugs.go` are of slice 13. `turnMixed` makes a key deliver an ORDER BY of mixed directions, `limitFirst` takes the rows of a LIMIT before the sort, `ascendLast` sorts by the last column of an ORDER BY ascending, `skipGap` makes a key (x, y, z) deliver ORDER BY x, z, and `lastIndexWins` makes the choice at run time take the indexes in the reverse of their order, so that of two that the rules rank alike the one created last wins. `TestDifferential_plantedBugs` plants them in the first 30, 12, 9, 30, and 12 sequences of the profile `order`, where 2, 6, 4, 4, and 7 find them by their own failure, and requires 1, 2, 1, 1, and 2. The own failure of the first four is the rows of an ordered select where SQLite has other rows; a bug that changes the choice is found by `pair.checkAccess` too, and a sort by the path that `pair.everyPath` forces. That of `lastIndexWins`, which changes nothing but the choice, is the failure of `pair.checkAccess`. `TestSimulation_plantedBugs` plants the first three in the hammer environment with the profile `order`, where 49, 82, and 118 of 200 seeds find them, each by the query that gives another answer than in SQLite. The environment has no crashes, so the seeds play out alike under the address sanitizer, which runs no crashes; in the default environment the sanitizer's job found `turnMixed` in 1 of 100 seeds.

   The generated tests and the simulation write values on the left since the generated checks of slice 12, of 2026-11-21: see the differential test, the fuzz targets, the simulation, and the planted bug `unturnedBound` above. One comparison in three has its value on the left in the mixed profile, and one in two in the profile `valuefirst` (`Profile.ValueFirst`). The fuzz targets that take their statements from the sequence write the form too: `FuzzDifferential`, and `FuzzSQL` in its seeds. Three checks are of slice 12:
   - **The counts.** `stmttest.ValueFirstCases` names each comparison with its value on the left, by its operator as the text writes it, and one with NULL. `reachValueFirst`, which `reachWhere` and `reachChange` call, counts each case by the outcome of its statement: "finds rows", "finds no row", or the refusal. It also counts the select, the update, or the delete with a value on the left by its own outcome, and `pair.reach` counts these again by the kind of the transaction. `TestDifferential` requires each kind, and `TestConn_differential` requires the kinds by write transaction and read-only transaction. The check of the driver counts the same in its own `reachValueFirst`.
   - **Every path, in the form of the text.** `stmttest.whereText` writes a condition with `ValueFirst` as the text does, with its `?` on the left of its column. `stmttest.SelectText`, `stmttest.FoundQuery`, and `stmttest.RowQueries` use it. So `pair.everyPath` runs each WHERE by every path in the form in which it came, and SQLite's side of the checks of an update runs that form too. Before slice 12, `whereText` wrote every condition with its column first. The rows of `TestDifferential.tsv` count 704 WHERE with a value on the left run by every path on 2026-11-21, 379 of them on a table with an index, where the 300 sequences before the profiles ran 497 and 195. The test counts those of their paths that a range bounds.
   - **The choice by a value on the left.** `checkChoice` counts a choice as "by a value on the left" when the model `stmttest.ChosenAccess` chooses another access with `!=` in place of the operator of each condition with its value on the left. `!=` pins and bounds nothing, and it keeps the numbers of the parameters and a comparison with NULL that is never true.

   The generated tests and the simulation write the defaults of slice 11 since its generated checks, of 2026-11-21: see the differential test, `FuzzTypes`, the simulation, and the planted bug `nullDefault` above. `stmttest.DefaultCases` names what an insert reaches of defaults. `TestDifferential`, `TestConn_differential`, by the kind of the transaction, and `TestGenerated` of `drivercheck` count the cases by outcome and require each.

   The generated tests and the simulation write the clauses of slice 10 since its generated checks, of 2026-11-20. One create and one drop in three says the clause in the mixed profile, and one in two in the profile `exists` (`Profile.Exists`). Three checks are of slice 10:
   - **The rule.** `stmttest.ExistsRule` gives why SQLite does nothing for a statement of the clause, from the tables and the indexes that SQLite has before the statement, and gives nothing where the statement does what the plain one does. The tests read the tables and the indexes from SQLite before each statement (`oracle.schema`). So the rule follows a roll back, and a drop table that took the indexes of its table along. `stmttest.IndexRule` gives no error for a statement that does nothing.
   - **The counts.** `reachExists` counts each statement of the clause as "did its work", as "did nothing:" with the reason of the rule, or by the kind of its error. It fails where the rule does nothing and the engine does not succeed. `TestDifferential` requires each kind, and `TestConn_differential` requires the kinds again by write transaction and read-only transaction. The check of the driver counts the same in its `pair.reachExists`.
   - **The simulation.** A statement that did nothing must be in no commit object and must change nothing in SQLite, and the planted bug `replaceExisting` shows that the checks find a statement that does something where SQLite does nothing; see the simulation and the planted bugs above. `FuzzIndexes` and `FuzzIndexes_core` write the clauses too; see above.

   The generated tests and the simulation create, read by, and drop indexes since the generated checks of slice 9, of 2026-11-20. A sequence of the mixed profile writes 3 create indexes and 1 drop index in 29 statements, on the columns of the table's shape in any order, each descending one time in three, with names that also meet the names of tables; one WHERE in two of a table on which it keeps an index pins leading columns of that index and bounds the next (`Sequence.indexConditions`). The profiles `indexes` and `valuefirst` have `Profile.Indexes` of 2: three in four of their create indexes are of every column but the key, and three in four of their WHERE of a table with an index pin its columns so. Four checks are of slice 9:
   - **Every path.** Before each call with WHERE, `pair.everyPath` runs its WHERE, as a select of its own (`stmttest.SelectText`, and `stmttest.SelectOf` for an update or a delete, whose rows are found by the same scan), on the second engine: by the rules, and by the paths of `candidates`, which are every row, the primary key, and each index of the table, each with every number of pinned columns that `Path.Limited` allows, of which the most bounds the next column, forward and backward, each once for what it reads. Each must give SQLite's rows as a multiset, and a forced path SQLite's rows of an `ORDER BY` of the key of its access (`accessKey`, `orderOf`), in that order. Where there are more than four candidates, it runs four at random from a seed of the pair's own (`pathSample`), so that the sequence draws as without them; the fuzz targets run every candidate. The rows of `TestDifferential.tsv` count 4,573 WHERE run so on 2026-11-21, 1,336 on a table with an index, and 1,130 by a sample, where the 300 sequences of 2026-11-20 ran 1,138, 443, and 349. The test counts each path by its kind (`pathKinds`): every row, the primary key, an index, by the number of pins and with or without a range, forward and backward, with a pin of NULL, with a range on a descending column, by an index of a descending column, and by an index that holds every column. The test requires each kind, so a run that stops reaching one fails.
   - **The choice against a model.** For every WHERE, `checkChoice` requires that `AccessOf` on the table and its indexes as SQLite has them is the access of `stmttest.ChosenAccess`, a second statement of the rules written from the roadmap's text and the decisions of the todo: which index, the pinned values, and the bounds, compared by value as SQLite compares them. It counts the choice by its kind.
   - **The plan of the cache.** One candidate in four, and the access of the rules one time in four, outside a transaction on the core, go through `core.ReadTx.ScanPlan`: SQLite's plan for the statement of the cache must be one row, `SEARCH` of the index of the access with a constraint for each pinned column and each bound, or `SCAN` of the index or of the table where it pins and bounds nothing, and no sort (`pair.checkPlan`, `wantPlan`). `TestEngine_rowsRead` checks the plan of each of its pinned queries the same way (`checkPlanOf`), so that its counts of rows read cannot hide SQLite filtering a whole index.
   - **The statements of indexes.** `reachIndex` checks each create index and drop index without arguments against `stmttest.IndexRule`, SQLite's errors of the names in SQLite's order, and counts each outcome, and for a create index that succeeds its number of columns, each type, a column that takes NULL, a descending column, and the primary key among them. The states that the tests compare hold the indexes of each table, with their columns and directions, on SQLite, on the core, and in `memory`, so every restart and every replay of a log with indexes, which the tests count, compares them.
   `TestDifferential_plantedBugs` plants `pinByPlace`, which makes `fit` look for the pin of the n-th column of a key among the conditions on the n-th column of the table, in both engines of the first 30 sequences of the profile `indexes`, and requires that 7 find it, a third of the 21 that did on 2026-11-21 (`differentialFind` gives the first difference of a sequence, and `instance.planted` turns the internal error that the bug can cause into an answer). Each sequence that finds it runs again without it, and must find nothing. The test plants `unturnedBound` in the same way in the first 30 sequences of the profile `valuefirst`, and requires that 5 find it, a third of the 15 that did, each by the rows of a select or by the count of an update or a delete, which differ from SQLite's. Of the first 60 sequences before the profiles, 11 and 6 found the two bugs. The simulation creates, reads by, and drops indexes too: every second engine of a node reads every row or forces a path of one of the sequence's index names, and the profiles `indexes` and `valuefirst` weigh the indexes; see the simulation and the planted bug `pinByPlace` above.

   The generated tests and the simulation write updates and deletes since the generated checks of slice 8, of 2026-11-20: see the differential test, the fuzz targets, the simulation, and the planted bugs `changeNothing` and `keepOldKey` above. The generated tests of the engine run every update and delete with WHERE on two engines, one for each way of reading rows. What is not built for slice 8 is in [the todo](../../docs/todo/slice-08.md).

   The generated tests and the simulation write WHERE since the generated checks of slice 7, of 2026-11-20: see the differential test, the fuzz targets, the simulation, and the planted bug `openBound` above. The generated tests of the engine read every select with WHERE both ways without a flag, and every second engine of a node of the simulation reads another way than the rules choose. `TestDifferential` and `FuzzDifferential_core` still take the flag `-scanall`, which makes every query read every row. What is not built for slice 7 is in [the todo](../../docs/todo/slice-07.md).

   The generated tests and the simulation write tables of one to three columns since 2026-11-20: see the differential test, the simulation, and the planted bug `listOrder` above. Since the generated checks of slice 6, of the same day, their tables have columns that take NULL, and their inserts give NULL and leave such columns out: see the same places, and the planted bug `zeroLeftOut`.
7. **No transaction left open.** `TestEngine_noneOpen` runs statements of a sequence, each through `Exec` or through `Query`. It consumes the rows of `Query` in one of four ways: to the end, with a loop that stops at once, with a close and no loop, or with a loop that panics. After each statement, a write transaction must begin, and a query on the connection of the instance must get its read transaction (`readFree`): it is a query of a table that does not exist, which gives SQLite's `no such table` once it has the transaction, and another error if a read transaction was left open. A second case plants `keepOpen`, and shows that a begin then waits.
8. **The names of the core.** `TestEngine_namesOfTheCore` runs the four statements on every name of the generator's pools and on random names in double quotes, of every kind of character. Wherever the pass supports the name, the core must take it, and the answer must be SQLite's.
9. **Concurrency.** `TestEngine_concurrent` runs inserts and queries from eight goroutines at once, each on a connection of its own, for the race detector. `TestConn` in `engine_test.go` runs the queries of twelve connections at once on the real core, each on the state of its start, and shows that a second query of a connection is refused while the rows of its first are open, that a write on that connection runs, and that a connection does not close inside the loop over the rows of its query.
10. **Transactions.** `tx_test.go` runs transactions that span statements on the real core with a simulated store, and `tx_internal_test.go` against a fake.

    | Test | What it shows |
    | --- | --- |
    | `TestConn_Begin` | Pinned sequences of statements in one transaction, on the engine and in SQLite. SQLite runs a write transaction between `begin immediate` and `commit` or `rollback`, and a read-only one under `pragma query_only = 1`, after `begin`. The test compares the answer and the count of changes after each statement, and the tables after the end. It compares the tables again after a replay of the log into an empty cache. Three of the sequences have NULL: one commits, one rolls back, and one is read-only. Three have updates and deletes: one commits, and keeps the transaction open after a change that fails; one rolls back; and one is read-only. |
    | `TestConn_netEffect` | The changes of the commit object of a transaction with updates and deletes, row by row, as the log holds them (`logChanges`). Nothing for a row that was inserted, updated, and deleted. The row as it is for a key that was changed away and back, and for a row that was deleted and inserted again. The new row and the delete of the old key for a change of the key. A row that an update found, also when its values did not change. Nothing of a statement that failed. For statements of their own, the commit objects and the annotations, no commit object for a statement that changes no row, and SQLite's tables after a replay of the log into an empty cache. |
    | `TestConn_differential` | 400 random sequences of 60 statements of the generator of the differential test: 20 of each profile, 40 of `mixed`, `exists`, `defaults`, and `valuefirst`, and 80 of `returning`, on other seeds of each profile than those of `TestDifferential`. The statements run in write transactions, in read-only ones, and on their own. Outside a transaction, one turn in `Profile.Transactions` begins one, two in five of them read-only, and each transaction ends with a commit or a roll back, at random. `pair.step` compares each statement as in `TestDifferential`, and runs it on a second engine that reads rows the other way, whose transactions begin and end with the first engine's. The test compares the tables of both engines with SQLite after the end of each transaction, and those of the first after a replay of the log. What each profile must reach is in `testdata/reached/TestConn_differential.kinds` and `.tsv`, as for `TestDifferential`: the selects that are read both ways and the updates and deletes with WHERE that are changed both ways, also in a read-only transaction, which refuses a change; and the commits and the roll backs of a write transaction whose update or delete changed rows, and of one with a create index or a drop index. On 2026-11-21 the profiles together read 2,002 selects both ways, 525 of them in a read-only transaction, and change 1,898 updates and deletes both ways, 465 of them in a read-only one, where the 60 sequences before the profiles gave 270, 64, 543, and 115. The test also requires the read-only error of an update and of a delete. For slice 10 it requires each of the four statements with a clause in a write transaction, where it did its work and where it did nothing, and in a read-only one, where it did nothing and where it was refused. It does not require a drop index with IF EXISTS that a read-only transaction refuses, which `TestEngine_exists` covers. For slice 11 it requires, in a write transaction, a create table with a default, an insert that stores a default, also that of the key, a NULL given for a column with a default, `UNIQUE constraint failed` from the default of the key, and `NOT NULL constraint failed` from DEFAULT NULL; and in a read-only one, the refusal of a create table with a default and of an insert that would store one. For slice 12 it requires, in a write transaction, a select with a value on the left that finds rows and one that finds none, an update and a delete with one that change rows, and an update with one that is refused for a value of another type; and in a read-only one, a select with one that finds rows and one that finds none, and the refusal of an update and of a delete with one. For slice 14 it requires an insert, an update, and a delete with RETURNING through `Query`, each where it succeeds in a write transaction and where a read-only one refuses it; the commit and the roll back of a transaction whose statement with RETURNING gave rows, after each of which the tables are compared; the rows of a RETURNING of an update and of a delete, each in the order of the key and in its reverse; an update that moves a row to another key, and `*` of an insert and of an update on a table with a column without a default after one with a default; and goqite's receive by id. For slice 15 it requires a select, an update, and a delete with a subquery that succeed in a write transaction, a select with one that succeeds in a read-only one and an update and a delete with one that it refuses, a subquery whose value was that of its one first row, and a subquery of an update or a delete that the engine did not compute. |
    | `TestConn_isolation` | Two or more connections. No other connection sees the writes of a transaction before its commit, and every read that starts after the commit sees them. A read-only transaction keeps the state of its beginning while others commit rows and tables. A read-only transaction and a write transaction run at once, and neither waits. A second write transaction begins only after the first has ended. A begin whose context ends while it waits gives the error of the context, and begins nothing. |
    | `TestConn_Commit` | The commit object of a transaction and its annotations, as the log holds them. No commit object for a transaction that changes nothing. A commit that is too large. |
    | `TestConn_rows` | The rules of [Rows in a transaction](#rows-in-a-transaction), for both kinds of transaction. Also `ErrTxOpen`, `ErrNoTx`, a begin on a closed connection, and the roll back at the close of a connection. |
    | `TestConn_stop` | The statements, the commit, and the roll back of a transaction that is open on a closed instance, and on an instance that has lost its lease. The lease is lost for real: the test cuts the store client of the instance off with `simstore.Client.CutOff`, and another instance takes the lease and commits. |
    | `TestConn_events` | The event of a transaction, the attribute `transaction.kind` of its statements, a commit that the instance refuses, a commit that is too large, and no value of an argument in any event. |
    | `TestConn_writeIn`, in `tx_internal_test.go` | The order of the calls `serving`, `savepoint`, `release`, and `rollbackTo` against a fake: for a write that succeeds, one that fails in its operator, one that fails in its annotation, and one that panics. A statement that is refused before its savepoint, or by it, which leaves the transaction open. A commit of unknown outcome, which ends the transaction and is logged at Warn. |

    A test of a wait does not depend on real time. It runs in a bubble of `testing/synctest`, whose clock moves only while every goroutine of the bubble waits. So a second writer that has not begun after `time.Sleep(time.Hour)` is waiting, and a clock that has not moved shows that no call waited.
11. **Mutation testing.** [`internal/sql/mutation`](../sql/mutation/README.md) mutates this package too, and runs the simulation as a layer of its own.

Under `-short`, the race detector, or the address sanitizer, `TestDifferential`, `TestEngine_noneOpen`, and `TestEngine_namesOfTheCore` run a tenth of their sequences, statements, and names, `TestConn_differential` runs one or two sequences of each profile, and `TestSimulation` runs a tenth of the seeds of each cell, one at least, and the first seed of three cells of each environment twice. The three generated tests check no count then. Nor does `TestSimulation` in a run of a part of its environments, with a `-run` filter on a subtest.

**The simulation runs no crashes under the address sanitizer.** A crashed database stays open until the test binary ends, because only the core's own tests can close the cache of a crashed instance. The sanitizer reports what is left open.

The flags of the test binary:

- `-seed`, if given, also as `-seed 0`, makes `TestDifferential` run the seeds from that one on, exactly as many as `-sequences` says, each with the profile of its seed, in place of the first seeds of each profile. It runs them all under `-short`, the race detector, and the address sanitizer too. The test then checks no count, and fails if no sequence ran. A sequence that fails logs its seed. The flag is also the seed of the sequence of `TestEngine_noneOpen`, and its default, 0, is a seed of the mixed profile.
- `-sequences` is the number of sequences of each profile of `TestDifferential`, which runs twice as many of `changes`, `indexes`, `exists`, `valuefirst`, `order`, `returning`, and `subquery`, and five times as many of `mixed` (`sequencesOf`). The default is 40, which gives 960 sequences, 200 of them of `mixed`. With more than the default, and without `-seed`, the sequences of the default run are among those run, and the test checks the least numbers alone (`Reached.Least`).
- `-length` is the number of statements in each sequence. The default is 48. With another length, with fewer sequences than the default, or with `-seed`, `TestDifferential` checks no count.
- `-scanall` makes each query of `TestDifferential` and `FuzzDifferential_core` read every row of its table, and not the rows that the rules choose. Every test that goes through `pair.step` reads rows both ways, with the flag and without it: `pair.bothWays` runs each call on a second engine, which reads another way.
- `-directions` is the seed of the directions of the queries of every instance of the tests. The default is 0, and each instance then draws a seed, except the instance of a sequence of `TestDifferential` and `TestConn_differential`, which derives its seed from the seed of the sequence (`newInstanceDirected`). Every engine of an instance has a seed: the engine of the n-th open of an instance has the seed of the instance plus n, unless its options have a seed. A test that fails logs the seed of each of its instances, with the flag that runs the test again in the same directions.
- `-replay` makes `TestSimulation` replay that one seed, and nothing else, and log its whole trace. `-workload` chooses the environment: `default`, the default, `hammer`, or `idle`. The profile is that of the seed.
- `-bug` plants the bug of that name in the replay.
- `-update` makes `TestAccessOf_golden` rewrite the golden files of the plans; see [The golden plans](#the-golden-plans).

A seed that fails in `TestSimulation` or `TestSimulation_plantedBugs` prints the command that replays it, with `-race` or `-asan` if the run that failed had one: the simulation runs no crashes under the address sanitizer, so a replay without it would be another run.

The environment variables:

- `S4QL_ENGINE_SIM_SEEDS` sets the number of seeds of each cell of each environment of `TestSimulation`, in place of the number of the cell. It is 1 at least. If it is at least the number of seeds of every cell, the largest of which is 40, the default seeds are among those run, and the test checks the least numbers alone (`Reached.Least`). With fewer, the test checks no count. For `TestSimulation_plantedBugs` it sets the limit of the seeds of the profile of each bug, 100 by default, and makes the test run every seed up to it, to count how many find each bug.
- `S4QL_RECOUNT=1` makes `TestDifferential`, `TestConn_differential`, and `TestSimulation` write their files of counts in `testdata/reached` from a full run, where they check the run against the files otherwise. A recount that finds a required kind in no run three times fails, and writes nothing. So does one in a run that cannot count: under `-short`, the race detector, or the address sanitizer, with `-seed`, with another number for `-sequences` or `-length`, with `S4QL_ENGINE_SIM_SEEDS`, or with a `-run` filter on a subtest of `TestSimulation`. Run it after a change of the generator, and read the diff of the files.

Examples of larger runs:

```sh
go test ./internal/engine/ -run '^TestDifferential$' -sequences 400
go test ./internal/engine/ -run '^TestDifferential$' -seed 1000000 -sequences 10000
go test ./internal/engine/ -run '^$' -fuzz '^FuzzDifferential$' -fuzztime 10m
go test ./internal/engine/ -run '^$' -fuzz '^FuzzDifferential_core$' -fuzztime 10m
S4QL_ENGINE_SIM_SEEDS=100 go test ./internal/engine/ -run '^TestSimulation$'
S4QL_ENGINE_SIM_SEEDS=200 go test ./internal/engine/ -run '^TestSimulation_plantedBugs$' -v
S4QL_RECOUNT=1 go test ./internal/engine/ -run '^(TestDifferential|TestConn_differential|TestSimulation)$'
go test -run '^TestSimulation$' -v ./internal/engine/ -replay 33 -bug skipUnique
```

The job `generated-engine` of the nightly workflow runs the first of these and the simulation with 100 seeds of each cell. Seed 33 is the second seed of the profile `tables`, on which `skipUnique` runs.

No test found a difference from SQLite, and the check of the driver found none from mattn's build.

The workload profiles, on 2026-11-21, with the profile `order` of slice 13 among them: `TestDifferential` runs 800 sequences of 48 statements and compares 34,042 calls with SQLite, where its 300 sequences before the profiles compared 12,108. `TestConn_differential` runs 300 sequences of 60 statements and compares 16,317 calls, where its 60 sequences compared 2,998. Each test fails if it compares fewer calls than before the profiles. `TestSimulation` runs 276 seeds in its three environments. No run found a difference from SQLite or a violation.

On that sequence, each slice that added statements took turns and random numbers from the others and moved every count: `openBound` fell from 32 seeds with the updates and deletes of slice 8 to 11 with slice 12, and `keepOldKey` from 29 to 17. `TestDifferential_plantedBugs` finds `pinByPlace` in 21 of its 30 sequences and `unturnedBound` in 15, where 11 and 6 of 60 sequences found them. The counts are counted again when the generator of a profile changes.

Mutation testing, on 2026-11-18 after the review: of 1,025 mutants, 308 do not compile, 703 are killed, none times out, and 12 are equivalent. The two that survived are killed by tests added after the run, which was checked by hand for each: the event of a syntax error through `Query`, and of a panic of a source before its first row. None is unexplained. An earlier run of this round left 10 survivors, each a gap in the tests, an equivalent swap of the arguments of `slices.Equal`, or code that a simpler form removed. See [`internal/sql/mutation`](../sql/mutation/README.md#the-pass-after-the-parser-and-the-engine). The code of slice 2 was mutated on 2026-11-19: of 77 mutants of the engine, 12 do not compile and 65 are killed; see [the parameters of slice 2](../sql/mutation/README.md#the-parameters-of-slice-2). The code of transactions was mutated the same day: of 628 mutants of `tx.go`, of the storage interface of transactions, and of the parts of `engine.go` that transactions changed, 128 do not compile, 469 are killed, 8 time out, and 23 are equivalent, after new tests of the events of transactions, of a statement whose context has ended, of a release that fails, and of a begin on a closed connection killed 14 survivors of the first run; see [the transactions of slice 3](../sql/mutation/README.md#the-transactions-of-slice-3).

### Run times

Measured on an Apple M4 with 10 cores, at a load of about 12 from other work:

- `go test ./internal/engine` takes about 8 s, `-short` about 2.5 s, and `-race` about 7 s. At a load of about 28, on 2026-11-18 after the review, it took 15 to 30 s.
- Under the address sanitizer, in a Linux container in Docker on the same machine, the tests of the engine took 89 s, and each fuzz test of `make fuzz` runs for `FUZZTIME` on top. `make test-asan FUZZTIME=10s` passed in 9 min 10 s while a mutation run used 6 cores.
- `TestDifferential` with 10,000 sequences took 266 s at a load of about 28, and `TestSimulation` with 1,000 seeds of each workload 233 s.
- On 2026-11-19, at a load of about 12 to 15, `TestSimulation` with its default 50 seeds of each workload took 3.2 to 3.3 s before the workloads had transactions and 3.9 to 4.1 s after, and 3.5 to 3.8 s before the idle workload and 5.2 to 5.5 s after it, at a load of about 12; `TestSimulation_plantedBugs` took 1.0 s with six bugs and 2.4 s with nine; and `TestSimulation` with 1,000 seeds of each workload took 75 s.
- On 2026-11-20, at a load of about 23, with tables of several columns in the generated tests, `go test ./internal/engine` took 21 s, and 27 s on the code before; `-short` took 14 s, and 11 to 15 s before. The time moves more with the load than with the change. `TestDifferential` with its default 300 sequences takes about 3.5 s, and `TestSimulation` with 1,000 seeds of each workload 150 s.
- On 2026-11-20, at a load of about 14 to 23, with the checks of slice 9 in the simulation (the integrity checks, the drops of an index under open rows, and the indexed sequences), `TestSimulation` with its default 50 seeds of each workload took 6.4 s, and 6.2 to 7.0 s on the code before; under the race detector 4.9 s, and 5.0 s before. With 1,000 seeds of each workload it took 137 s, and 280 s at a load of about 24.
- With the clauses of slice 10 in the generated tests, at a load of about 25 to 30, `go test ./internal/engine` took 178 s of wall time and 72 s of user time, and 134 s and 74 s on the code before. The user time is about the same, so the wall time moves with the load. `TestSimulation` with 1,000 seeds of each workload took 340 s at that load.
